Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do your concerns about your face, particularly the left side, ever change or do you always think it looks the same way?\n\nPatient: It… kind of always comes back to the same thing, if that makes sense. Like, I’ll tell myself maybe it’s just the camera angle or the lighting, but then I see an ID photo or my online class camera and I’m right back to thinking the left side of my face looks off—my nose and jaw look crooked.\n\nI know people say they don’t see it, so I’m trying not to make it sound worse than it is, but to me it feels pretty constant… like everyone in the CNC lab would notice that before anything else.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself repeatedly checking your face in mirrors or other reflective surfaces?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Probably more than I should.\n\nBefore I leave for school I can get kind of stuck in the bathroom mirror, and then I’ll check my phone camera too, just to see if it looks as bad from that angle. I’ll fix my hair or change shirts, like maybe it’ll hide the side of my face better. It sounds kind of dumb when I say it out loud.\n\nSometimes I ask my mom if it looks weird before I go, and she says it’s fine, but I don’t really feel settled. And sometimes I do it for so long that I’m late or I miss lab, or I get there and I’m already so drained I can’t really focus.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have to perform specific rituals or behaviors to try and reduce your anxiety about your face?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I do, if that counts as rituals. I don’t know if I’d call them that, but before I leave for school I can get stuck checking the bathroom mirror, then my phone camera, kind of over and over.\n\nI’ll fix my hair or change shirts to try to make that side of my face look less obvious, or hide the angle better. And sometimes I ask my mom, like, “Does it look weird?” even though I know that probably gets annoying.\n\nIt’s embarrassing to say, but sometimes it takes so long that I miss lab, or I get there and I’m already so worn out I can’t really focus. I’m not trying to be dramatic… that’s just kind of what happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to repeat these checking behaviors a specific number of times to feel okay about your face?\n\nPatient: Not really a specific number, no. It’s more like… I keep checking until it feels a little less wrong, I guess.\n\nLike before school I’ll look in the bathroom mirror, then my phone camera, then maybe fix my hair or change shirts to hide that angle better. And sometimes I’ll ask my mom if it looks weird, even though I know she’s probably tired of me asking.\n\nI don’t count it out or anything. It just… keeps going longer than I mean it to, and then sometimes I miss lab or I get there so wiped out I can’t focus. I don’t know if that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think something bad would happen if you didn't check your face in the mirror or camera before leaving the house?\n\nPatient: I don’t think something bad would happen exactly… not like in a superstitious way or anything. It’s more that if I don’t check, I feel like I’m walking out not knowing how bad it looks, and then I can’t stop thinking about it.\n\nSo I’ll check the bathroom mirror, then my phone camera, and kind of try to fix my hair or change shirts so that angle isn’t as obvious. Sometimes I’ll ask my mom if it looks weird, even though I know she’s just going to say it’s fine.\n\nI know that probably sounds excessive. I just feel like I need to do it to calm down enough to leave, but sometimes it goes on too long and I end up missing lab or showing up already exhausted and unable to focus.\n\nDoctor: Are you trying to make your face look perfect when you check it, or are you just trying to make the anxiety about how it looks go away?\n\nPatient: I think… mostly I’m trying to get the anxiety to back off. I don’t really expect it to look perfect, exactly. It’s more like I see that angle of my face and it feels wrong, and then I need to check again or fix my hair or change my shirt so it’s not as noticeable.\n\nBefore school is the worst, because I’ll go from the bathroom mirror to my phone camera, and sometimes I’ll ask my mom if it looks weird. I know that probably sounds like a lot. I’m not trying to be vain or anything… I just feel like I can’t leave until I’ve calmed down a little.\n\nSometimes I do it too long and then I miss lab, or I get there already wiped out and can’t really focus. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: When you're checking your face and trying to fix how it looks, are you trying to make it look symmetrical?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably part of it. I don’t know if I’d say I’m trying to make it “perfectly” symmetrical, but the thing I keep getting stuck on is that the left side of my face looks off to me.\n\nLike in ID photos or on the online class camera, my nose and jaw look crooked, and then I can’t unsee it. Even when people tell me they don’t notice anything, I still feel like everyone in the CNC lab is seeing that first, before they notice my work or anything I’m doing.\n\nI know that might sound kind of… dramatic. But yeah, I guess I’m trying to make it look less uneven, or at least less obvious. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Is it always the same specific features, like your nose and jaw, that you're concerned about when you think your face looks off?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s mostly the same things. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m picking apart every little thing, but it’s usually the left side of my face that I get stuck on.\n\nEspecially my nose and jaw. In ID photos or on the camera for online class, it just looks crooked to me, like that side is… off. And even when people say they don’t really see it, I still feel like when I’m in the CNC lab, that’s what people notice first before anything else, like before my work or what I’m doing. I know that might not make total sense, but that’s how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Is it the specific appearance of your nose and jaw that bothers you, or is it more about how they fit into the overall look of your face?\n\nPatient: I think it’s… kind of both, but more how they make the whole left side of my face look, if that makes sense.\n\nLike, I focus on my nose and jaw because those are the parts that seem crooked to me in ID photos or on the online class camera. But it’s not just “my nose” by itself. It’s that when I see them together, the whole side of my face looks off, and then I feel like that’s what people are noticing.\n\nI know people have told me they don’t really see it, so I’m trying not to overstate it, but it’s honestly a big reason I came in. In the CNC lab I’ll feel like everyone notices that before they notice my work or anything else about me. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Is it the idea that your face is imperfect in general that bothers you, or is it specifically the crookedness of your nose and jaw on the left side?\n\nPatient: It’s more specific than just feeling like my face is imperfect in general, I think.\n\nI mean… I’m sure I notice other things sometimes, but the thing I really can’t stop coming back to is the left side. Like my nose and jaw look crooked to me, especially in ID photos or on the online class camera. And then once I see it, I feel like everyone else must see it too.\n\nThat’s honestly kind of why I came in. Even if people tell me they don’t notice it, when I’m in the CNC lab I feel like that’s what people are seeing before they notice my work or anything else. I know that probably sounds like I’m making too much of it, but it feels hard to shake.\n\nDoctor: Is it the presence of this specific flaw, like the crookedness of your nose and jaw, that bothers you, or would any noticeable imperfection in your face be just as disturbing?\n\nPatient: I think it’s this specific thing, mostly. Like… I don’t want to say I’d be totally fine with any other flaw, but the thing I came in about is that I can’t stop thinking the left side of my face looks off.\n\nIt’s the nose and jaw looking crooked to me, especially in ID photos or on the online class camera. And then even if people say they don’t see it, I still feel like everyone in the CNC lab is noticing that before they notice my work. I know that probably sounds like a lot, but that’s the part that really gets stuck in my head.\n\nDoctor: Would you be just as bothered by any other noticeable imperfection in your face, or is it specifically the crookedness of your nose and jaw that disturbs you?\n\nPatient: It’s specifically the crookedness, I think. I mean, I don’t want to sound like I’m saying every other thing about my face is perfect or anything, but that’s the part I can’t stop checking or thinking about.\n\nIt’s like the left side of my face just looks off to me—my nose and jaw especially. In ID photos or on the online class camera it feels really obvious, and then even when people tell me they don’t see it, I still feel like people in the CNC lab are noticing that before they notice my work. I know that might sound exaggerated, but that’s honestly what bothers me the most.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your concerns about your face are mostly about fixing this one specific issue with your nose and jaw, or is it more about achieving a general sense of perfection in your appearance?\n\nPatient: Mostly fixing that one thing, I think. I mean, I don’t want to act like I’m trying to be perfect overall or anything… it’s more that I came in because I can’t stop thinking the left side of my face looks off.\n\nLike my nose and jaw look crooked to me in every ID photo or on the online class camera, and even when people say they don’t see it, I still feel like everyone in the CNC lab is noticing that before they notice my work. I know that probably sounds a bit much, but that’s the part I keep getting stuck on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face would look okay if the left and right sides were symmetrical, even if they didn't look perfectly 'normal'?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I’m not trying to look perfect or anything, at least I don’t think I am. It’s more that I can’t stop seeing the left side as off, like my nose and jaw are crooked.\n\nIf they were symmetrical, I feel like I could probably relax more, even if my face wasn’t, you know, ideal or whatever. It’s just… in ID photos or on the online class camera, that crooked look is what jumps out at me, and then I start thinking people in the CNC lab notice that before anything else. I know that might sound kind of extreme, sorry, but that’s how it feels.\n\nDoctor: Would any flaw that makes your face asymmetrical bother you as much as the crookedness of your nose and jaw?\n\nPatient: I guess… not just any flaw, no. I don’t want to sound too picky or like I’m obsessing over everything, but the thing I really get stuck on is the left side looking off—like my nose and jaw are crooked.\n\nThat’s what I came in about, really. In ID photos or on the online class camera, it just jumps out at me, and even when people say they don’t see it, I still feel like people in the CNC lab notice that before they notice my work. I know that probably sounds exaggerated, but that’s the part that bothers me the most.\n\nDoctor: Would you be bothered by any other flaw that makes your face asymmetrical, or is it just the crookedness of your nose and jaw that disturbs you?\n\nPatient: I think it’s mostly that specific crookedness, yeah. I mean, maybe if there was some other asymmetry I’d notice it, but the thing I can’t stop thinking about is the left side of my face looking off—my nose and jaw, especially.\n\nIt shows up to me in every ID photo or on the online class camera, and then I start worrying people in the CNC lab see that before they see my work. I know that probably sounds like I’m making too big a deal out of it, but that’s really the part that disturbs me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
